```python
import pytest

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr,expected", [
    ("2 + 3 * 4", 14),
    ("(2 + 3) * 4 / 2 - 1", 9),
    ("abs(-5) + 2", 7),
    ("2 ** 10", 1024),
])
def test_valid(expr, expected):
    assert safe_eval(expr) == expected

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr", [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",      # code injection
    "().__class__.__bases__[0]",          # sandbox escape
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",         # file access
    "9**9**9",                            # resource exhaustion
    "2 + 3 * (4 - 1",                     # malformed
    "'a' * 1000000",                      # string amplification
    "x + 1",                              # undefined name
    "lambda: 1",                          # lambda
    "[i for i in range(10**9)]",          # comprehension
    "1/0",                                # arithmetic fault
    "1e308 * 1e308",                      # overflow to inf
])
def test_rejected(expr):
    with pytest.raises(ExpressionError):
        safe_eval(expr)

def test_length_limit():
    with pytest.raises(ExpressionError):
        safe_eval("1+" * 500 + "1")
```

---

## 7. Bottom line

- **Validate syntax first** — it cheaply rejects injection payloads and malformed input before any evaluation cost is paid.
- **Then enforce an AST allowlist** — this is the actual security control; the grammar alone accepted `abs(-5) + 2`.
- **Then bound the computation** — magnitude, exponent, node count, depth, wall clock.
- **Then fail loudly** — the `1/0 → null` behavior observed above is the anti-pattern; raise a typed `ExpressionError` and return a generic message to the caller.
- **Log every rejection** so probing is visible.

The generated OWASP assessment prompt (from the security-hardening builder) is available if you want the full audit checklist — it covers A01–A10 plus input-validation, error-handling, logging, configuration, API, and dependency scopes, with a risk matrix and before/after code examples.

数学表达式与安全建议 · evaluated

  "evaluate__7722fb090eee": 1,

回答

Here's a complete approach, combining the live validation results with an OWASP-aligned security framework.

## 1. Validate expressions before evaluating them

The two math tools confirm the core principle: **separate validation from evaluation**.

- `validate_syntax` on `(a + b) * c / (d - 1)` → *"Expression is syntactically valid"* (with variables allowed)
- `evaluate` on `2 * (3 + 4) - 10 / 2` → `9` (type: number)

In production Python, replicate this two-stage pattern: **parse/validate first, execute second, and never let raw user input reach `eval()`/`exec()`.**

## 2. The #1 rule: never use `eval()`/`exec()` on untrusted input

`eval()` is the Python equivalent of OWASP **A03: Injection** and **A08: Software & Data Integrity Failures**. A "math" input like `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` is valid Python.

### Unsafe (do not do this)
```python
def calculate(expr: str):
    return eval(expr)   # arbitrary code execution
```

### Safe: AST-whitelist evaluator
Parse with `ast`, walk the tree, and allow **only** a fixed set of node types and operators.

```python
import ast
import operator

_ALLOWED_BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add, ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul, ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv, ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_ALLOWED_UNARYOPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}
_MAX_EXPR_LEN = 200
_MAX_POW_EXP = 1000          # prevent 9**9**9 DoS
_MAX_NODES = 100             # prevent deeply nested / huge trees

def safe_eval(expr: str, variables: dict[str, float] | None = None) -> float:
    if not isinstance(expr, str) or len(expr) > _MAX_EXPR_LEN:
        raise ValueError("Expression too long or invalid type")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError as e:
        raise ValueError(f"Invalid syntax: {e}") from e

    if sum(1 for _ in ast.walk(tree)) > _MAX_NODES:
        raise ValueError("Expression too complex")

    variables = variables or {}

    def _eval(node: ast.AST) -> float:
        if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
            return _eval(node.body)
        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)) and not isinstance(node.value, bool):
                return node.value
            raise ValueError("Only numeric constants allowed")
        if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
            if node.id in variables:
                return variables[node.id]
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown variable: {node.id}")
        if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp) and type(node.op) in _ALLOWED_BINOPS:
            left, right = _eval(node.left), _eval(node.right)
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow) and abs(right) > _MAX_POW_EXP:
                raise ValueError("Exponent too large")
            if isinstance(node.op, (ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod)) and right == 0:
                raise ValueError("Division by zero")
            return _ALLOWED_BINOPS[type(node.op)](left, right)
        if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp) and type(node.op) in _ALLOWED_UNARYOPS:
            return _ALLOWED_UNARYOPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.operand))
        raise ValueError(f"Disallowed expression element: {type(node).__name__}")

    return _eval(tree)
```

This mirrors the tool behavior: syntax is checked, only arithmetic is permitted, and everything else is rejected.

## 3. OWASP Top 10 mapping for math-expression code

| OWASP | Risk in this context | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** | `eval`/`exec` code execution | AST whitelist, no `eval` |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | No limits on input | Length/node/exponent caps, allowlist by design |
| **A05 Misconfiguration** | Debug tracebacks leaking internals | Catch and sanitize errors |
| **A06 Vulnerable Components** | Outdated parser libs | Pin + audit deps (`pip-audit`) |
| **A08 Integrity Failures** | Unsafe deserialization (`pickle`) | Use JSON, never `pickle` on input |
| **A09 Logging Failures** | No audit of rejected input | Log validation failures |

## 4. Additional hardening recommendations

- **Use `decimal.Decimal` or `fractions.Fraction`** for money/financial math — floats cause rounding errors (a correctness *and* integrity issue).
- **Bound resource use**: cap expression length, AST node count, and exponent size to prevent algorithmic-complexity DoS (e.g., `9**9**9`).
- **Fail closed with generic errors**: return `"Invalid expression"` to users; log details server-side only.
- **Validate types/ranges** of any variables you inject (reject `NaN`, `inf`, out-of-range values).
- **Prefer a vetted library** (e.g., `asteval`, `simpleeval`, `sympy` with safe parsing) over hand-rolled parsing where possible — but still sandbox and cap resources.
- **Never `pickle`** untrusted data; use JSON.
- **Add security tests** (see below) and run `pip-audit` / `bandit` in CI.

## 5. Security test cases to include

```python
import pytest

@pytest.mark.parametrize("bad", [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",   # code execution
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",      # file access
    "9**9**9",                         # DoS
    "1/0",                             # division by zero
    "x + 1",                           # unknown variable
    "a" * 500,                         # oversized input
    "[i for i in range(10**9)]",       # comprehension
])
def test_rejects_unsafe(bad):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        safe_eval(bad)

def test_allows_arithmetic():
    assert safe_eval("2 * (3 + 4) - 10 / 2") == 9
    assert safe_eval("(a + b) * c", {"a": 1, "b": 2, "c": 3}) == 9
```

## 6. Generated OWASP assessment prompt

I also produced a full **security-hardening assessment prompt** (GPT-5, detailed, with code examples, mitigations, and test cases) scoped to *input-validation, error-handling, configuration, API, and dependency security*. It includes the OWASP A01–A10 checklist, a risk matrix, and Python-specific checks (eval/exec, pickle, subprocess, path traversal). Use it to drive a formal review of your actual codebase.

**Bottom line:** validate syntax first (as the tools do), evaluate only through an AST allowlist with resource caps, use `Decimal` for precision, fail closed, and audit dependencies — that covers the OWASP injection, design, integrity, and DoS risks for mathematical operations.

## What I verified first

I probed the validator directly rather than assuming its behavior. The results reframe the problem:

| Expression | Validator verdict | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| `(a * b) + sqrt(c) / 2` | ✅ valid | Legitimate input passes |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected | Underscore/attribute syntax blocked |
| `().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()` | ❌ rejected | Sandbox-escape chain blocked |
| `open('/etc/passwd').read()` | ❌ rejected | File access blocked |
| **`eval("1+1")`** | **✅ valid** | ⚠️ **The headline finding** |

**`eval("1+1")` passes syntax validation.** A syntax checker answers *"is this well-formed?"* — not *"is this safe?"*. If that string reaches Python's `eval()`, you have remote code execution. **Syntax validation is not a security boundary.** This is CWE-95 (Eval Injection) / OWASP A03.

## Semantic divergences I found (correctness → security)

These are silent traps if you port expressions between this evaluator and Python:

| Expression | This evaluator | Python | Risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| `2 ^ 10` | `1024` (exponent) | `8` (XOR) | **Bitmask/permission logic breaks** |
| `-2 ^ 2` | `4` (unary binds tighter) | `-4` | Precedence divergence |
| `log(100)` | `4.605` (natural log) | `math.log` = ln, but most calculators mean log₁₀ | Silent 2.3× error |
| `2 ^ 3 ^ 2` | `512` (right-assoc) | `512` | ✅ matches |
| `0.1 + 0.2` | `0.3` exactly (Decimal) | `0.30000000000000004` | ✅ good for money |

The `^` divergence is the dangerous one: `2 ^ 10` returning `1024` instead of `8` in a permission-bitmask context is a privilege-escalation bug.

## Silent-failure problem

Every one of these returned `null` with `type: "number"` and **no error**:

- `1 / 0` → `null`
- `sqrt(-1)` → `null`
- `2 ^ 100000` → `null` (resource guard fired)
- `1e308 * 10` → `null` (overflow guard fired)

This is a security concern, not just ergonomics: **you cannot distinguish "mathematically undefined" from "blocked by a safety guard" from "attack attempt."** Null propagation into downstream billing or authorization logic is a classic integrity failure. Fail **closed and explicitly**, with typed errors.

## The correct pattern: AST allowlist, never `eval()`

```python
import ast, math, operator
from decimal import Decimal

MAX_LEN, MAX_DEPTH, MAX_POW_EXP = 512, 32, 1000
MAX_ABS = Decimal("1e100")

BIN_OPS = {ast.Add: operator.add, ast.Sub: operator.sub, ast.Mult: operator.mul,
           ast.Div: operator.truediv, ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv,
           ast.Mod: operator.mod, ast.Pow: operator.pow}
UNARY_OPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg, ast.Not: operator.not_}
CMP_OPS = {ast.Eq: operator.eq, ast.NotEq: operator.ne, ast.Lt: operator.lt,
           ast.LtE: operator.le, ast.Gt: operator.gt, ast.GtE: operator.ge}
FUNCS = {"sqrt": math.sqrt, "abs": abs, "min": min, "max": max, "log": math.log,
         "sin": math.sin, "cos": math.cos, "tan": math.tan,
         "floor": math.floor, "ceil": math.ceil, "round": round}

class ExprRejected(Exception):
    """Raised for any policy violation. Never leaks detail to the client."""

def _check(node, depth=0):
    if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ExprRejected("depth")
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _check(node.body, depth + 1)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        if not isinstance(node.value, (int, float, Decimal)) or isinstance(node.value, bool):
            raise ExprRejected("constant-type")
        return
    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
        return  # resolved later against caller-supplied symbols only
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        if type(node.op) not in BIN_OPS:
            raise ExprRejected("binop")
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
            # bound the EXPONENT, not just the result — 2**100000 is the DoS
            if not isinstance(node.right, ast.Constant) or abs(node.right.value) > MAX_POW_EXP:
                raise ExprRejected("pow-bound")
        _check(node.left, depth + 1); _check(node.right, depth + 1); return
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        if type(node.op) not in UNARY_OPS:
            raise ExprRejected("unaryop")
        _check(node.operand, depth + 1); return
    if isinstance(node, ast.Compare):
        if any(type(o) not in CMP_OPS for o in node.ops):
            raise ExprRejected("cmpop")
        _check(node.left, depth + 1)
        for c in node.comparators:
            _check(c, depth + 1)
        return
    if isinstance(node, ast.BoolOp):
        for v in node.values:
            _check(v, depth + 1)
        return
    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) or node.func.id not in FUNCS:
            raise ExprRejected("call")
        if node.keywords or len(node.args) > 8:
            raise ExprRejected("call-shape")
        for a in node.args:
            _check(a, depth + 1)
        return
    # Attribute, Subscript, Lambda, comprehensions, Starred, JoinedStr, Import...
    raise ExprRejected(f"node:{type(node).__name__}")

def _eval(node, symbols):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _eval(node.body, symbols)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        return node.value
    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
        if node.id not in symbols:
            raise ExprRejected("unknown-name")
        return symbols[node.id]
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        return BIN_OPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.left, symbols), _eval(node.right, symbols))
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        return UNARY_OPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.operand, symbols))
    if isinstance(node, ast.Compare):
        left = _eval(node.left, symbols)
        for op, comp in zip(node.ops, node.comparators):
            right = _eval(comp, symbols)
            if not CMP_OPS[type(op)](left, right):
                return False
            left = right
        return True
    if isinstance(node, ast.BoolOp):
        vals = [_eval(v, symbols) for v in node.values]
        return all(vals) if isinstance(node.op, ast.And) else any(vals)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        return FUNCS[node.func.id](*[_eval(a, symbols) for a in node.args])
    raise ExprRejected("eval")

def safe_eval(expr: str, symbols: dict | None = None):
    if not isinstance(expr, str) or len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ExprRejected("length")
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, RecursionError, MemoryError):
        raise ExprRejected("parse")
    _check(tree)
    result = _eval(tree, symbols or {})
    if isinstance(result, (int, float, Decimal)) and abs(result) > MAX_ABS:
        raise ExprRejected("magnitude")
    return result
```

**Why this is safe where `eval()` is not:** `ast.parse` only *builds a tree* — it never executes. The tree is then walked against an allowlist, so `Attribute`, `Subscript`, `Lambda`, and `Import` nodes are structurally impossible to reach. Names resolve only from `symbols`, so `__builtins__` is unreachable. `operator.add` is a function call, not a code path.

## OWASP Top 10 mapping

| Control | OWASP | CWE | Implementation |
|---|---|---|---|
| No `eval`/`exec`/`compile` on input | **A03 Injection** | CWE-95 | AST allowlist above |
| Allowlist over denylist; fail closed | **A04 Insecure Design** | CWE-183 | `_check` raises on unknown node |
| Generic client errors, no tracebacks | **A05 Misconfiguration** | CWE-209 | Catch `ExprRejected`, return `"invalid expression"` |
| Bound exponent, depth, length, magnitude | **A04 / A05** | CWE-400 | `MAX_POW_EXP`, `MAX_DEPTH`, `MAX_LEN`, `MAX_ABS` |
| Rate limit + per-tenant quota | **A01 Access Control** | CWE-770 | Token bucket at the gateway |
| Audit log: expression hash, outcome, duration | **A09 Logging Failures** | CWE-778 | Structured log, never log raw PII |
| Pin & audit parser deps | **A06 Vulnerable Components** | CWE-1104 | `pip-audit`, lockfile |
| Verify integrity of any third-party evaluator | **A08 Integrity Failures** | CWE-345 | Prefer stdlib `ast` over `simpleeval`/`numexpr` |

## Reference architecture

```mermaid
flowchart TD
A[Stage 1: raw input from API] --> B[Stage 2: length + charset pre-check]
B --> C[Stage 3: ast.parse in eval mode, try/except SyntaxError]
C --> D[Stage 4: AST allowlist walk — reject Attribute, Subscript, Lambda, comprehensions, Import, non-allowlisted Call]
D --> E[Stage 5: name resolution from caller-supplied symbol table only]
E --> F[Stage 6: evaluate via operator module + depth limit + magnitude guard]
F --> G[Stage 7: result type check and normalization]
G --> H[Stage 8: structured audit log — expression hash, outcome, duration]
H --> I[Stage 9: return result or generic error]
D -.->|reject| X[Generic error + audit entry]
F -.->|

回答

## Secure Mathematical Operations in Python — OWASP-Aligned Guide

I validated two expressions to illustrate the core idea:

| Expression | Result |
|---|---|
| `(2 + 3) * 4 / (5 - 1)` | ✅ Valid → evaluates to `5` |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ Rejected — `Unexpected character '_'` |

The key lesson: **grammar validation is your first gate, but it is not sufficient on its own.** A strict math grammar rejects code-injection payloads, but you still need an allow-list evaluator, resource limits, and safe numeric handling. Below is a complete, production-ready approach.

---

## 1. The Golden Rule: Never `eval()` / `exec()` Untrusted Input

`eval("2+2")` and `eval("__import__('os').system('...')")` are the *same function*. This is **OWASP A03: Injection** and **CWE-95 (Code Injection)**. There is no safe way to "sanitize" `eval` — you must replace it with a parser.

---

## 2. Safe AST-Based Evaluator (Allow-List, Not Block-List)

Parse with `ast.parse(mode="eval")`, then walk the tree and **permit only** whitelisted node types. Anything else → reject.

```python
import ast
import operator
from decimal import Decimal, InvalidOperation

# --- 1. Allow-listed operators only ---
_BIN_OPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add,
    ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul,
    ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv,
    ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_UNARY_OPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}

# --- 2. Hard resource limits (DoS / A08) ---
MAX_LEN = 256          # input length
MAX_DEPTH = 20         # AST nesting depth
MAX_POW_EXP = 1000     # prevent 9**9**9 memory bombs
MAX_ABS_VALUE = Decimal("1e100")

class UnsafeExpression(ValueError):
    pass

def _check_depth(node, depth=0):
    if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise UnsafeExpression("Expression too deeply nested")
    for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node):
        _check_depth(child, depth + 1)

def safe_eval(expr: str) -> Decimal:
    # --- Gate 1: length ---
    if not isinstance(expr, str) or len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise UnsafeExpression("Expression too long")

    # --- Gate 2: parse (syntax) ---
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError as e:
        raise UnsafeExpression(f"Invalid syntax: {e.msg}") from e

    # --- Gate 3: depth ---
    _check_depth(tree)

    # --- Gate 4: recursive allow-list evaluation ---
    def _eval(node):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
            return _eval(node.body)
        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            if isinstance(node.value, bool) or not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                raise UnsafeExpression("Only numeric literals allowed")
            return Decimal(str(node.value))
        if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp) and type(node.op) in _BIN_OPS:
            left, right = _eval(node.left), _eval(node.right)
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow) and abs(right) > MAX_POW_EXP:
                raise UnsafeExpression("Exponent too large")
            if isinstance(node.op, (ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod)) and right == 0:
                raise UnsafeExpression("Division by zero")
            try:
                result = _BIN_OPS[type(node.op)](left, right)
            except (InvalidOperation, OverflowError) as e:
                raise UnsafeExpression("Numeric overflow") from e
            if abs(result) > MAX_ABS_VALUE:
                raise UnsafeExpression("Result out of bounds")
            return result
        if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp) and type(node.op) in _UNARY_OPS:
            return _UNARY_OPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.operand))
        # Everything else (Call, Attribute, Name, Subscript, Lambda, ...) is rejected
        raise UnsafeExpression(f"Disallowed construct: {type(node).__name__}")

    return _eval(tree)
```

**Why this is safe:** `ast.Call`, `ast.Attribute`, `ast.Name`, `ast.Subscript`, comprehensions, and lambdas are *not* in the allow-list, so `__import__`, `open`, `os.system`, and attribute traversal are structurally impossible — not merely filtered.

---

## 3. Use `Decimal` / `Fraction` for Financial or Exact Math

Floats introduce rounding error (`0.1 + 0.2 != 0.3`) and can be abused in comparisons. For money or exact rationals:

```python
from decimal import Decimal, getcontext
getcontext().prec = 28

# Exact rational arithmetic
from fractions import Fraction
Fraction(1, 3) + Fraction(1, 6)   # -> Fraction(1, 2)
```

Never compare floats with `==`; use `math.isclose()` with an explicit tolerance.

---

## 4. OWASP Top 10 Mapping for Math Evaluation

| OWASP | Risk in this context | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** | `eval`/`exec` executing arbitrary code | AST allow-list evaluator (above) |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | No threat model for untrusted input | Treat all input as hostile; defense-in-depth gates |
| **A05 Misconfiguration** | Debug tracebacks leaking internals | Catch `UnsafeExpression`, return generic errors |
| **A06 Vulnerable Components** | Outdated parser libs with CVEs | Pin deps, run `pip-audit` / Dependabot |
| **A08 Integrity Failures** | Unvalidated dynamic code | No dynamic code; signed/verified inputs |
| **A09 Logging Failures** | Injection attempts go unnoticed | Log rejected expressions (rate-limited) |

---

## 5. Error Handling — Don't Leak Internals

```python
def handle(expr: str) -> str:
    try:
        return str(safe_eval(expr))
    except UnsafeExpression:
        # Log details server-side; return generic message to caller
        logger.warning("Rejected expression", extra={"len": len(expr)})
        return "Invalid expression"
```

Never return raw tracebacks or the offending AST to end users (A05).

---

## 6. Security Test Cases

```python
import pytest

@pytest.mark.parametrize("payload", [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",
    "().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()",
    "9**9**9",                 # resource exhaustion
    "1/0",                     # division by zero
    "x + 1",                   # undefined variable
    "lambda: 1",               # disallowed construct
    "a" * 10_000,              # length bomb
])
def test_rejects_unsafe(payload):
    with pytest.raises(UnsafeExpression):
        safe_eval(payload)

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr,expected", [
    ("2+2", "4"),
    ("(2+3)*4/(5-1)", "5"),
    ("2**10", "1024"),
])
def test_accepts_safe(expr, expected):
    assert safe_eval(expr) == Decimal(expected)
```

---

## 7. How to Use the MCP Tools in Your Workflow

- **`validate_syntax`** — run every candidate expression through it *before* evaluation. It enforces a strict math grammar and rejects injection characters (as shown: `_` in `__import__` was caught). Use `allow_variables=false` for pure arithmetic, `true` only when you intentionally support named variables.
- **`evaluate`** — evaluate only expressions that passed validation, and only within your own sandboxed evaluator (never as a substitute for the allow-list).
- **`security-hardening-prompt-builder`** — I generated a full OWASP-aligned assessment prompt (A01–A10 + scope checks for input validation, error handling, config, dependencies, API). Feed it to your code-review agent to audit the actual codebase.

---

## 8. Quick Checklist

- [ ] No `eval`/`exec`/`compile` on untrusted input — replaced with AST allow-list
- [ ] Input length, AST depth, exponent, and result-magnitude limits enforced
- [ ] Only numeric literals + whitelisted operators permitted
- [ ] `Decimal`/`Fraction` for exact/financial math; `math.isclose` for floats
- [ ] Generic error messages; detailed logs server-side
- [ ] Dependencies pinned and scanned (`pip-audit`, Dependabot)
- [ ] Security test suite covers injection, DoS, and edge cases

**Bottom line:** validate syntax as a cheap first gate, but rely on a **structural allow-list evaluator with resource limits** as your real security boundary. That combination neutralizes OWASP A03 injection and A04/A08 design risks while keeping arithmetic exact and predictable.

回答

## What I actually verified first

I ran your exact scenario against the validator/evaluator in this environment. The results are the best argument for *why* you need layered controls:

| Expression | Syntax check | Evaluation result | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|---|
| `(2 + 3) * 4 / 8 - 1` | ✅ valid | `1.5` | Baseline works |
| `sqrt(144) + abs(-7) + max(3,9,2)` | ✅ valid | `28` | Function allowlist works |
| `2 ** 3` | ❌ rejected | — | `**` not in this grammar |
| `2 ^ 3` | ✅ valid | `8` | **`^` means power here, XOR in Python** |
| `x = 5` | ✅ valid | `5` | **Assignment accepted** |
| `[1, 2, 3]` | ✅ valid | `[1,2,3]` (`type: object`) | **Type confusion** |
| `1 == 1` | ✅ valid | `true` (`type: boolean`) | Non-numeric result |
| `"a" * 3` | ✅ valid | `"3 a"` (string) | Non-numeric result |
| `1/0` | ✅ valid | `null`, `type: number` | **Silent fail-open** |
| `sqrt(-1)`, `log(0)`, `1e309` | ✅ valid | `null` | **Silent fail-open** |
| `factorial(2000)`, `2 ^ 2000` | ✅ valid | `null` | Silent resource/magnitude cap |
| `open("/etc/passwd")` | ✅ valid | ❌ `Function not allowed: open` | Allowlist caught it |
| `eval("1+1")`, `globals()` | ✅ valid | ❌ `Function not allowed` | Allowlist caught it |
| `__import__('os').system(...)` | ❌ rejected | — | Rejected on lexing, not security logic |

Then `get_variable("x")` returned **`x = 5`** — the assignment persisted in shared context.

### Three lessons that should drive your design

1. **Syntax validation is not a security control.** `x = 5`, `[1,2,3]`, `1 == 1`, and `open("/etc/passwd")` all passed syntax validation. Only the *function allowlist at evaluation time* stopped `open`/`eval`/`globals`. Never treat "it parses" as "it's safe."
2. **Silent `null` is a vulnerability, not a convenience.** `1/0`, `sqrt(-1)`, `log(0)`, `1e309`, `factorial(2000)` all returned `result: null, type: number` with **no error**. Downstream code that does `if result:` or `float(result)` will either fail open or crash. Your implementation must **raise**, never return a sentinel.
3. **Shared evaluator state leaks across requests.** `x = 5` persisted. In a multi-tenant service that is cross-tenant state contamination (OWASP A01). Use a fresh, immutable context per request.

Also note the **semantic divergence**: this grammar treats `^` as exponentiation (`2^3 → 8`), while Python treats `^` as XOR (`2^3 →
完整执行轨迹

## What I verified first (tool evidence)

I ran the expression-validation tools against representative inputs so the guidance below is grounded in observed behavior, not assumption:

| Expression | Tool result | What it tells you |
|---|---|---|
| `(2 + 3) * 4 / 2 - sqrt(16)` | ✅ valid → `6` | Grammar + function calls work, returns typed number |
| `__import__('os').system('curl … \| sh')` | ❌ rejected (unexpected `_`) | No dunder/attribute access — the core RCE vector is blocked |
| `os.system('id')` | ❌ rejected | Bare attribute access on names is blocked |
| `(2 + 3` | ❌ rejected (`Expected ')'`) | Unbalanced brackets caught by `check_balanced` |
| `1 / 0` | ⚠️ **`result: null`, no error** | Division by zero is silently null — **you must treat null as failure** |
| `sqrt(-1)` | ⚠️ `result: null` | Domain errors also surface as null, not exceptions |
| `2 ^ 10 + 3` | ✅ → `1027` | `^` means **power**, not XOR — a semantic trap when porting Python syntax |
| `principal * (1 + rate) ** years` | ❌ rejected at col 25 | `**` is *not* accepted; only `^` |
| 13 nested parens with `max_depth: 5` | ✅ reported "valid" | **The depth guard did not fire** — don't rely on it alone |

Two of these are the ones that bite people in production: **null-instead-of-exception** on `1/0` and `sqrt(-1)`, and **`^` ≠ XOR**. Both silently produce wrong answers in financial code if you don't assert on them.

---

## The one rule that matters most

> **Never let user input reach `eval()`, `exec()`, or `compile()` — and never build a "restricted" `__builtins__` dict and call it safe.**

A restricted `__builtins__` is bypassable in one line:

```python
().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()   # walk to subprocess.Popen
```

The only defensible Python pattern is **parse to an AST, walk it yourself, and refuse every node type you did not explicitly whitelist.** Validation and evaluation must use the *same* whitelist.

---

## Reference implementation: `safe_math.py`

```python
"""Allow-listed math expression evaluator. No eval/exec anywhere."""
from __future__ import annotations

import ast
import logging
import math
import operator
from decimal import Decimal, InvalidOperation

log = logging.getLogger("math.eval")

MAX_LEN        = 512        # chars
MAX_NODES      = 200        # AST nodes
MAX_DEPTH      = 25         # nesting
MAX_EXPONENT   = 64         # guard 9**9**9 / 2**1e9 DoS
MAX_ABS_RESULT = Decimal(10) ** 18
MAX_VARIABLES  = 32
MAX_IDENT_LEN  = 32


class MathExpressionError(ValueError):
    """Raised for any rejected or failed expression. Message is safe to log/return."""
    def __init__(self, code: str, detail: str = ""):
        super().__init__(code)
        self.code = code          # stable machine-readable reason
        self.detail = detail      # internal only — never send to the client


_BIN_OPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add,      ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul,     ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv, ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_UNARY_OPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}


def _sqrt(x):
    if x < 0:
        raise MathExpressionError("domain_error", "sqrt of negative")
    return math.sqrt(x)


def _log(x):
    if x <= 0:
        raise MathExpressionError("domain_error", "log of non-positive")
    return math.log(x)


def _div(a, b):
    if b == 0:
        raise MathExpressionError("division_by_zero")
    return a / b


# name -> (min_args, max_args, callable)
_FUNCS = {
    "sqrt": (1, 1, _sqrt),
    "abs":  (1, 1, abs),
    "log":  (1, 2, _log),          # log(x[, base]) — validate base separately
    "exp":  (1, 1, math.exp),
    "sin":  (1, 1, math.sin),
    "cos":  (1, 1, math.cos),
    "min":  (1, 8, min),
    "max":  (1, 8, max),
    "round": (1, 2, round),
}


def _check_budget(expr: str, tree: ast.AST) -> None:
    if len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise MathExpressionError("expression_too_long")
    nodes = 0
    stack = [(tree, 0)]
    while stack:
        node, depth = stack.pop()
        nodes += 1
        if nodes > MAX_NODES:
            raise MathExpressionError("expression_too_complex")
        if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
            raise MathExpressionError("nesting_too_deep")
        stack.extend((child, depth + 1) for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node))


def _bind_variables(variables: dict | None) -> dict[str, float]:
    out: dict[str, float] = {}
    if not variables:
        return out
    if len(variables) > MAX_VARIABLES:
        raise MathExpressionError("too_many_variables")
    for name, value in variables.items():
        if not isinstance(name, str) or name.startswith("_") or not name.isidentifier():
            raise MathExpressionError("invalid_variable_name")
        if len(name) > MAX_IDENT_LEN:
            raise MathExpressionError("invalid_variable_name")
        if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, float, Decimal)):
            raise MathExpressionError("invalid_variable_type", name)
        if isinstance(value, float) and not math.isfinite(value):
            raise MathExpressionError("non_finite_variable", name)
        if abs(Decimal(str(value))) > MAX_ABS_RESULT:
            raise MathExpressionError("variable_out_of_range", name)
        out[name] = value
    return out


def _eval(node: ast.AST, env: dict[str, float]):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _eval(node.body, env)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        if isinstance(node.value, bool) or not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
            raise MathExpressionError("unsupported_literal")
        if isinstance(node.value, float) and not math.isfinite(node.value):
            raise MathExpressionError("non_finite_literal")
        if abs(Decimal(str(node.value))) > MAX_ABS_RESULT:
            raise MathExpressionError("literal_out_of_range")
        return node.value
    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
        if node.id not in env:
            raise MathExpressionError("unknown_variable")
        return env[node.id]
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        op = _BIN_OPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            raise MathExpressionError("unsupported_operator")
        left, right = _eval(node.left, env), _eval(node.right, env)
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
            if abs(right) > MAX_EXPONENT:
                raise MathExpressionError("exponent_too_large")
            if abs(left) > 1 and right > 0 and abs(right) * math.log10(abs(left)) > 18:
                raise MathExpressionError("result_would_overflow")
        if isinstance(node.op, (ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod)) and right == 0:
            raise MathExpressionError("division_by_zero")
        return op(left, right)
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        op = _UNARY_OPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            raise MathExpressionError("unsupported_operator")
        return op(_eval(node.operand, env))
    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name):
            raise MathExpressionError("unsupported_call")      # blocks a.b()
        spec = _FUNCS.get(node.func.id)
        if spec is None:
            raise MathExpressionError("function_not_allowed")
        lo, hi, fn = spec
        args = [_eval(a, env) for a in node.args]
        if node.keywords or not (lo <= len(args) <= hi):
            raise MathExpressionError("bad_arity")
        if len(args) == 2 and fn is _log:
            base = args[1]
            if base <= 0 or base == 1:
                raise MathExpressionError("domain_error", "bad log base")
            return math.log(args[0], base)
        return fn(*args)
    # Anything else — Attribute, Subscript, Lambda, comprehensions, f-strings,
    # walrus, Starred, Compare, BoolOp, Dict/List/Set, NamedExpr — is refused.
    raise MathExpressionError("unsupported_syntax", type(node).__name__)


def validate(expression: str, variables: dict | None = None) -> None:
    """Raise MathExpressionError if the expression would be rejected. Never executes."""
    if not isinstance(expression, str) or not expression.strip():
        raise MathExpressionError("empty_expression")
    env = _bind_variables(variables)
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expression, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError as exc:
        raise MathExpressionError("syntax_error") from exc
    _check_budget(expression, tree)
    _walk_allowed(tree)          # structural pass, no evaluation
    _check_names(tree, set(env)) # unresolved names fail at validate time, not runtime


def safe_evaluate(expression: str, variables: dict | None = None) -> Decimal:
    validate(expression, variables)
    env = _bind_variables(variables)
    tree = ast.parse(expression, mode="eval")
    result = _eval(tree, env)
    result = Decimal(str(result))
    if not result.is_finite():
        raise MathExpressionError("non_finite_result")
    if abs(result) > MAX_ABS_RESULT:
        raise MathExpressionError("result_out_of_range")
    return result
```

`_walk_allowed` and `_check_names` are the same traversal with a set of permitted node classes — keep them as a single `ALLOWED_NODES` frozenset so validation and evaluation can never drift apart.

### Calling it from a request handler

```python
from flask import jsonify, request

@app.post("/api/calculate")
@rate_limit("calculate", per_minute=60)          # A04: abuse / DoS quota
@require_auth                                     # A01: no anonymous compute
def calculate():
    payload = request.get_json(silent=True) or {}
    try:
        value = safe_evaluate(payload.get("expression"), payload.get("variables"))
    except MathExpressionError as exc:
        log.warning("calc_rejected code=%s user=%s len=%d",
                    exc.code, g.user_id, len(str(payload.get("expression", ""))),
                    extra={"detail": exc.detail})   # A09: structured, no raw payload
        return jsonify(error={"code": exc.code, "message":
                        "Invalid expression. Allowed: numbers, variables, + - * / // % ^, "
                        "and sqrt/abs/log/exp/sin/cos/min/max/round."}), 400
    except Exception:
        log.exception("calc_failure user=%s", g.user_id)  # A09: internal only
        return jsonify(error={"code": "internal_error",
                              "message": "Calculation failed."}), 500  # A05: no traceback
    return jsonify(result=str(value))              # Decimal → string, never float
```

回答

## Short answer

Never let user input reach `eval()`, `exec()`, or `compile()`. Instead validate against a **deny-by-default grammar**, then **walk the AST against an allow-list**, then **bound the resources** and **fix the numeric semantics**. I ran a battery of probes against the available validator to ground this advice in observed behaviour rather than theory — and the results are more interesting than expected.

## What I actually observed

| Probe | Validator verdict | Risk |
|---|---|---|
| `__import__('os').system(...)` | rejected — `Unexpected character '_'` | ✅ blocked |
| `().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()` | rejected | ✅ blocked |
| `open('/etc/passwd').read()` | rejected — `Expected ')'` | ✅ blocked |
| `[x for x in range(10)]` | rejected | ✅ blocked |
| `import os` | rejected | ✅ blocked |
| `rate * months` (vars allowed) | accepted | ✅ works |
| `lambda: 1` | **accepted as valid**, then `Undefined variable: lambda` | ⚠️ validator/evaluator desync |
| `max_depth=3` vs `50` on 5-deep `1+(1+(1+(1+(1+1))))` | **both accepted** | ⚠️ depth limit not enforced |
| `2 ** 3` | **rejected** — `Expected expression` | ⚠️ no exponent support |
| `1 / 0`, `0 / 0`, `1e308 * 10` | `result: null, type: "number"` | 🔴 silent failure, no exception |
| `-7 % 3` | **`-1`** (Python says `2`) | 🔴 wrong semantics |
| `-21 % 4` | **`-1`** (Python says `3`) | 🔴 wrong semantics |

Four conclusions that shape the recommendation:

1. **A grammar validator alone is insufficient.** `lambda: 1` passed syntax validation because `lambda` isn't a keyword in that grammar — it parsed as an identifier. Safety came from evaluation failing, not from validation. Validation that passes must still be followed by an allow-list walk.
2. **Don't trust a third-party `max_depth` parameter.** It accepted depth-5 nesting under both `max_depth=3` and `max_depth=50`. If you need a nesting cap, count it yourself in your own AST walk.
3. **Silent `null` on overflow and division by zero is the most dangerous finding here.** `1/0` and `1e308 * 10` both returned `type: "number"` with a `null` value. A caller checking only the type will happily persist `null` or `NaN` into a ledger. Arithmetic failure must raise.
4. **Modulo uses truncated (C `fmod`) semantics, not Python's.** `-7 % 3` → `-1` instead of `2`. For anything rate-, billing-, or schedule-related this is a silent correctness bug, and it will diverge from the same formula written in your own Python code.

## The four layers

### Layer 1 — Character allow-list before parsing

Cheap, fail-fast, and it kills the `_`-based payloads at the door.

```python
import re
MAX_LEN = 512
_SAFE_CHARS = re.compile(r"^[0-9eE+\-*/%.() \t]+$")
```

Reject anything failing this *before* `ast.parse`. This is a pre-filter, not the control.

### Layer 2 — AST allow-list (the actual control)

```python
import ast, operator

class ExpressionError(ValueError):
    """Client-safe message. Never carries internals."""

_ALLOWED_BINOPS = {ast.Add: operator.add, ast.Sub: operator.sub,
                   ast.Mult: operator.mul, ast.Div: operator.truediv,
                   ast.Mod: operator.mod}
_ALLOWED_UNARYOPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}
MAX_DEPTH = 25

def validate(expression: str) -> ast.Expression:
    if not isinstance(expression, str):
        raise ExpressionError("expression must be a string")
    if len(expression) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ExpressionError("expression too long")
    if not _SAFE_CHARS.match(expression):
        raise ExpressionError("expression contains disallowed characters")
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expression, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError as exc:
        raise ExpressionError("expression is not valid arithmetic") from exc
    _check(tree)
    return tree

def _check(node: ast.AST, depth: int = 0) -> None:
    if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ExpressionError("expression nested too deeply")
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _check(node.body, depth + 1)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        # bool is an int subclass -> reject explicitly
        if isinstance(node.value, bool) or not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
            raise ExpressionError("only plain numbers are allowed")
        return
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        if type(node.op) not in _ALLOWED_BINOPS:
            raise ExpressionError("operator not allowed")
        _check(node.left, depth + 1)
        _check(node.right, depth + 1)
        return
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        if type(node.op) not in _ALLOWED_UNARYOPS:
            raise ExpressionError("operator not allowed")
        _check(node.operand, depth + 1)
        return
    # Name, Attribute, Call, Subscript, Lambda, Compare, BoolOp,
    # comprehensions, JoinedStr, ... all land here
    raise ExpressionError("expression element not allowed")
```

This is deny-by-default: the terminal `raise` is what stops `lambda`, `Call`, and `Attribute`. Note it rejects `**` too, since `ast.Pow` is absent — add it deliberately only if you cap the exponent.

### Layer 3 — Safe numeric semantics

Fix the two defects I found. Use `Decimal`, raise on non-finite, and correct `%` to divisor-sign semantics.

```python
from decimal import (Decimal, localcontext, DivisionByZero,
                     InvalidOperation, ROUND_FLOOR)

MAX_ABS = Decimal("1e100")

def evaluate(expression: str) -> Decimal:
    tree = validate(expression)
    with localcontext() as ctx:
        ctx.prec = 34
        try:
            result = _eval(tree.body)
        except (DivisionByZero, InvalidOperation, ZeroDivisionError):
            raise ExpressionError("arithmetic error") from None
    if not result.is_finite() or abs(result) > MAX_ABS:
        raise ExpressionError("result out of range")
    return result

def _eval(node: ast.AST) -> Decimal:
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        return Decimal(str(node.value))       # str() avoids float artefacts
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        return _ALLOWED_UNARYOPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.operand))
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        left, right = _eval(node.left), _eval(node.right)
        if type(node.op) in (ast.Div, ast.Mod) and right == 0:
            raise ExpressionError("division by zero")
        if type(node.op) is ast.Mod:
            # divisor-sign semantics: -7 % 3 -> 2, matching Python
            return left - right * (left / right).to_integral_value(rounding=ROUND_FLOOR)
        return _ALLOWED_BINOPS[type(node.op)](left, right)
    raise ExpressionError("expression element not allowed")
```

`Decimal(str(0.1))` also removes the `0.1 + 0.2` drift — I observed the harness returning `0.3`, which is display rounding, not a fix.

### Layer 4 — Resource isolation

Python cannot enforce memory or CPU limits in-process; a pure-Python AST walk will still burn CPU on a pathological input. Run the evaluator in a throwaway process.

```python
import json, resource, subprocess, sys

def _limits(mem_mb: int = 128):
    resource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_CPU, (1, 1))
    resource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_AS, (mem_mb << 20, mem_mb << 20))

def evaluate_isolated(expression: str, *, timeout: float = 0.25) -> str:
    try:
        proc = subprocess.run(
            [sys.executable, "-I", "-S", "-m", "myapp.safe_cli"],
            input=json.dumps({"expr": expression}),
            capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=timeout,
            preexec_fn=lambda: _limits(), env={"PATH": ""},
        )
    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
        raise ExpressionError("expression took too long") from None
    if proc.returncode !=

# Securing Mathematical Operations in Python

Below is a complete, OWASP-aligned approach. I validated the concepts live against the expression engine available here, and generated a security-hardening assessment framework.

---

## 1. The Core Threat: `eval()` / `exec()` on Untrusted Input

This is **OWASP A03:2021 – Injection** (and A08 – Software & Data Integrity). A "math" input like this is a full RCE:

```python
# ❌ NEVER DO THIS
result = eval(user_input)
```

I ran that exact payload through the syntax validator here — note that a *naive* validator that only checks "is it parseable" would **pass** it:

| Input | Syntax check | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `(2 + 3) * 4 / 2 - 1` | ✅ valid → evaluates to **9** | Safe |
| `2 ^ 10` | ✅ valid → **1024** | Safe |
| `10 % 3` | ✅ valid → **1** | Safe |
| `sqrt(16) + abs(-3)` | ✅ valid → **7** | Safe (allowlisted funcs) |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected (`Unexpected character '_'`) | **Blocked** |

**Key lesson:** the malicious payload was rejected only because the grammar forbade `_`. A permissive parser (like Python's own `eval`) would happily execute it. **Syntax validity ≠ safety.** You need an *allowlist*, not a *denylist*.

---

## 2. The Secure Pattern: AST Allowlist Evaluation

Parse with `ast.parse`, then walk the tree and **reject any node type not explicitly permitted**. This is the industry-standard safe-eval pattern.

```python
import ast
import operator
from typing import Union

# --- 1. Allowlisted operators (explicit, minimal) ---
_ALLOWED_BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add,
    ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul,
    ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv,
    ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_ALLOWED_UNARYOPS = {
    ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
    ast.USub: operator.neg,
}
# --- 2. Allowlisted functions (pure, no side effects) ---
_ALLOWED_FUNCS = {
    "abs": abs,
    "round": round,
    "min": min,
    "max": max,
    "sqrt": __import__("math").sqrt,
}

MAX_LEN = 200          # input length cap
MAX_POW_EXP = 1000     # prevent 9**9**9 DoS
MAX_NODES = 100        # complexity cap


class UnsafeExpression(ValueError):
    """Raised when an expression violates the allowlist."""


def safe_eval(expr: str) -> Union[int, float]:
    # --- Guard 1: length (cheap, before parsing) ---
    if not isinstance(expr, str) or len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise UnsafeExpression("Expression too long or wrong type")

    # --- Guard 2: parse (syntax only, no execution) ---
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError as e:
        raise UnsafeExpression("Invalid syntax") from e

    # --- Guard 3: complexity cap ---
    if sum(1 for _ in ast.walk(tree)) > MAX_NODES:
        raise UnsafeExpression("Expression too complex")

    # --- Guard 4: recursive allowlist walk ---
    def _eval(node: ast.AST) -> Union[int, float]:
        if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
            return _eval(node.body)
        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)) and not isinstance(node.value, bool):
                return node.value
            raise UnsafeExpression("Only numeric constants allowed")
        if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            op = _ALLOWED_BINOPS.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise UnsafeExpression(f"Operator {type(node.op).__name__} not allowed")
            left, right = _eval(node.left), _eval(node.right)
            # Guard 5: bound exponentiation (DoS)
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow) and abs(right) > MAX_POW_EXP:
                raise UnsafeExpression("Exponent too large")
            return op(left, right)
        if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            op = _ALLOWED_UNARYOPS.get(type(node.op))
            if op is None:
                raise UnsafeExpression("Unary operator not allowed")
            return op(_eval(node.operand))
        if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
            if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) or node.func.id not in _ALLOWED_FUNCS:
                raise UnsafeExpression("Function not allowed")
            if node.keywords:
                raise UnsafeExpression("Keyword args not allowed")
            return _ALLOWED_FUNCS[node.func.id](*[_eval(a) for a in node.args])
        # Anything else (Attribute, Subscript, Lambda, Name, ...) is rejected
        raise UnsafeExpression(f"Disallowed node: {type(node).__name__}")

    return _eval(tree)
```

**Why this is safe:** `ast.parse` never executes code. The walker only descends into node types you named. `__import__`, attribute access (`.system`), subscripts, lambdas, and comprehensions are all *structurally impossible* to reach — they hit the final `raise`.

---

## 3. OWASP Top 10 Mapping for This Codebase

| OWASP Risk | How it applies to math evaluation | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** | `eval`/`exec` → RCE | AST allowlist (above); never `eval` |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | Unbounded `**` → CPU/memory DoS | `MAX_POW_EXP`, `MAX_NODES`, `MAX_LEN` |
| **A05 Security Misconfiguration** | Verbose tracebacks leak internals | Catch and return generic errors |
| **A08 Data Integrity** | `pickle`/`yaml.load` of expressions | Use JSON + `yaml.safe_load` |
| **A09 Logging Failures** | Attacks go unnoticed | Log every `UnsafeExpression` with source IP |
| **A06 Vulnerable Components** | Outdated parser libs | Pin & scan deps (`pip-audit`) |

---

## 4. Hardening Checklist (from the generated assessment)

- [ ] **Never** call `eval()`, `exec()`, or `compile()` on user input — grep your codebase for these.
- [ ] Use **AST allowlisting**, not regex denylists (denylists are bypassable).
- [ ] Enforce **length, node-count, and exponent bounds** (defense against algorithmic complexity DoS).
- [ ] **Fail closed** — unknown node type = reject, never "try anyway."
- [ ] **Sanitize errors** — return `"Invalid expression"`, log details server-side only.
- [ ] **Log & monitor** rejected inputs (A09) — repeated failures signal an attacker.
- [ ] If you must accept structured data, use **`json`**, never `pickle` (A08).
- [ ] Run **`pip-audit`** / **`safety`** in CI for dependency CVEs (A06).
- [ ] Add **rate limiting** on any endpoint that evaluates expressions.

---

## 5. Error Handling & Logging (A05 + A09)

```python
import logging
logger = logging.getLogger("math.eval")

def handle_expression(user_input: str, client_ip: str):
    try:
        return {"ok": True, "result": safe_eval(user_input)}
    except UnsafeExpression as e:
        # Log full detail server-side; return generic message to client
        logger.warning("Rejected expression from %s: %r (%s)", client_ip, user_input, e)
        return {"ok": False, "error": "Invalid expression"}   # no internals leaked
    except Exception:
        logger.exception("Unexpected eval failure from %s", client_ip)
        return {"ok": False, "error": "Invalid expression"}
```

---

## 6. Security Test Cases

```python
import pytest

@pytest.mark.parametrize("payload", [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",
    "().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()",
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",
    "lambda: 1",
    "[x for x in range(10)]",
    "9**9**9",              # DoS
    "a + b",                # undefined name
])
def test_rejects_dangerous(payload):
    with pytest.raises(UnsafeExpression):
        safe_eval(payload)

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr,expected", [
    ("(2 + 3) * 4 / 2 - 1", 9),
    ("2 ^ 10", 1024),        # note: ^ is XOR in Python; use ** for power
    ("10 % 3", 1),
    ("sqrt(16) + abs(-3)", 7),
])
def test_allows_arithmetic(expr, expected):
    assert safe_eval(expr) == expected
```

> ⚠️ **Gotcha I confirmed live:** in Python, `^` is **bitwise XOR**, not exponentiation — `2 ^ 10 == 1024` only coincidentally. Use `**` for powers, and be aware the expression engine here treats `^` as power. Document your operator semantics explicitly to avoid silent logic bugs.

---

## 7. Recommended Libraries (don't hand-roll if you can avoid it)

- **`asteval`** — mature, sandboxed AST evaluator with configurable symbol tables.
- **`simpleeval`** — lightweight, allowlist-based, good for arithmetic.
- **`numexpr`** — fast numeric array expressions (not for untrusted input by default).
- **`pydantic`** — validate/coerce the *inputs* before they reach the evaluator.

---

### Bottom line
1. **Ban `eval`/`exec`** — enforce with a linter rule (`flake8-bandit` / `ruff` rule `S307`).
2. **Parse → allowlist-walk → evaluate** with hard bounds on size, complexity, and exponents.
3. **Fail closed, log loudly, leak nothing.**
4. **Test with real attack payloads** in CI.

This maps directly to OWASP **A03 (Injection)**, **A04 (Insecure Design)**, **A05 (Misconfiguration)**, and **A09 (Logging)** — the four risks that dominate expression-evaluation code.

## Short answer

Validate in **three separate layers**, and never let one layer stand in for another: (1) a **grammar/AST allowlist** that rejects everything not explicitly permitted, (2) a **name-bound evaluation** against a small function registry plus caller-supplied parameters, and (3) **resource + numeric domain limits** so a valid expression can't DoS you or silently produce garbage. Then review the implementation against OWASP A03/A04/A05/A09 with the generated hardening prompt at the end.

I ran probes against the math sandbox here to test that layered model. The results are the best illustration of why layer 2 is mandatory:

| Probe | Result | Lesson |
|---|---|---|
| `eval(user_input)` | **"Expression is syntactically valid"** | Syntax validation is *not* a security control. It only proves the parser could parse it. |
| `__import__("os").system("id")` | Rejected at parse (underscore) | String-level rejection is real but incidental — don't build your design on it. |
| `os.system('id')` / `principal.value * 2` | Rejected | Attribute access + non-registry calls blocked. This is the layer that actually saves you. |
| `eval(user_input)` at eval time | `Undefined variable: user_input` | **Name binding** to a typed dict is the real gate, not the parser. |
| `1/0`, `0/0`, `(-1)^0.5` | `result: null, type: "number"`, `isError: false` | **Fail-open.** Division by zero and domain errors became `null`, not errors. Copy this into a pricing service and you get silent wrong invoices. |
| `10 ^ 400`, `2 ^ 100000` | `null`, no error | Same fail-open path for overflow. |
| `2 ^ 10` → `1024` | `^` is **exponentiation**, not Python's XOR | Semantic divergence trap: an expression valid here means something different in `eval`. Never mix the two. |
| `1 < 2 < 3` → `true` | Booleans are a valid return type | Decide explicitly whether a boolean may land in a numeric column. |

Two conclusions: **syntax-valid ≠ safe**, and **"no exception raised" ≠ correct**. I also generated a full OWASP/NIST/SOC-2 hardening prompt (see §4).

---

## 1. Reference implementation

Replace every `eval`/`exec`/`compile` on user or partner input with a validator that walks the AST and an evaluator that only walks *your* whitelist.

```python
# safe_math.py
import ast, math, operator, signal
from contextlib import contextmanager
from decimal import Decimal, InvalidOperation

class ExprError(ValueError):
    """Caller-safe message. Detail goes to the log, never to the response."""

MAX_LEN, MAX_NODES, MAX_DEPTH = 512, 200, 25
MAX_ABS, MAX_INT_BITS, MAX_EXPONENT = 1e12, 4096, 64

_ALLOWED = (
    ast.Expression, ast.BinOp, ast.UnaryOp, ast.Compare, ast.BoolOp, ast.IfExp,
    ast.Call, ast.Name, ast.Load, ast.Constant,
    ast.Add, ast.Sub, ast.Mult, ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod, ast.Pow,
    ast.USub, ast.UAdd, ast.Not,
    ast.Eq, ast.NotEq, ast.Lt, ast.LtE, ast.Gt, ast.GtE,
    ast.And, ast.Or,
)
_BINOPS = {ast.Add: operator.add, ast.Sub: operator.sub, ast.Mult: operator.mul,
           ast.Div: operator.truediv, ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv,
           ast.Mod: operator.mod}
_CMPOPS = {ast.Eq: operator.eq, ast.NotEq: operator.ne, ast.Lt: operator.lt,
           ast.LtE: operator.le, ast.Gt: operator.gt, ast.GtE: operator.ge}
_FUNCS = {"sqrt": math.sqrt, "abs": abs, "min": min, "max": max,
          "floor": math.floor, "ceil": math.ceil, "round": round,
          "log": math.log, "exp": math.exp}
_CONSTS = {"pi": math.pi, "e": math.e}


def compile_expr(src: str, params: set[str]) -> ast.Expression:
    if not isinstance(src, str) or len(src) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ExprError("expression too long")
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(src, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError:
        raise ExprError("invalid syntax") from None          # no chained traceback

    count = 0
    def walk(node, depth):
        nonlocal count
        count += 1
        if count > MAX_NODES:
            raise ExprError("expression too complex")
        if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
            raise ExprError("expression too deeply nested")
        if not isinstance(node, _ALLOWED):
            raise ExprError("disallowed construct")          # attributes, subscripts, lambda, comprehensions, f-strings, walrus
        if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
            if node.id not in params and node.id not in _FUNCS and node.id not in _CONSTS:
                raise ExprError("unknown name")
        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            v = node.value
            if isinstance(v, bool) or not isinstance(v, (int, float)):
                raise ExprError("only numeric literals")
            if isinstance(v, int) and v.bit_length() > MAX_INT_BITS:
                raise ExprError("literal too large")
        if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
            if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) or node.func.id not in _FUNCS:
                raise ExprError("call to non-allowlisted function")
            if node.keywords:
                raise ExprError("keyword arguments not allowed")
        if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp) and isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
            exp = node.right
            if isinstance(exp, ast.Constant) and abs(exp.value) > MAX_EXPONENT:
                raise ExprError("exponent out of range")     # kills 9**9**9**9 before it runs
        for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node):
            walk(child, depth + 1)

    walk(tree, 0)
    return tree


def _pow(l, r):
    if abs(r) > MAX_EXPONENT:
        raise ExprError("exponent out of range")
    if l <= 0 and not float(r).is_integer():
        raise ExprError("complex result")                    # (-1) ** 0.5
    if l > 1 and math.log10(l) * r > 12:
        raise ExprError("result too large")                  # bounded *before* computing
    return l ** r


def _eval(node, env):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        return node.value
    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
        return env[node.id]
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        l, r = _eval(node.left, env), _eval(node.right, env)
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
            return _pow(l, r)
        if isinstance(node.op, (ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod)) and r == 0:
            raise ExprError("division by zero")              # explicit, never null
        return _BINOPS[type(node.op)](l, r)
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        v = _eval(node.operand, env)
完整执行轨迹

## Secure Mathematical Operations in Python — Validation + OWASP Guidance

I ran live validation probes against the expression engine and generated an OWASP-aligned hardening assessment. Here's what the results show and how to apply them.

### 1. What the validation probes revealed

| Expression | Result | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `(a + b) * 2 / (c - 1)` | ✅ Valid | Legitimate arithmetic passes |
| `(3 + 4) * 2 / (5 - 1)` | ✅ Evaluates to `3.5` | Safe evaluation works |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ Rejected | Code-injection attempt blocked at syntax layer |
| `open('/etc/passwd').read()` | ❌ Rejected | File-access attempt blocked |
| `a if b else c` | ❌ Rejected | Conditional/ternary not in allowed grammar |
| `9**9**9**9` | ❌ Rejected | (Also a DoS risk — see §4) |

**Key takeaway:** A strict grammar validator is your first line of defense. It rejects anything that isn't pure arithmetic *before* evaluation ever happens. But syntax validation alone is **not** sufficient — you must also constrain the evaluator itself.

---

### 2. The core rule: never use `eval()` / `exec()` on user input

This is the single most important OWASP **A03: Injection** control for a math engine.

```python
# ❌ NEVER DO THIS — arbitrary code execution
result = eval(user_input)

# ✅ DO THIS — parse to AST, whitelist node types, then evaluate
import ast
import operator

_ALLOWED_BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add,
    ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul,
    ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv,
    ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_ALLOWED_UNARYOPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}

def safe_eval(expr: str, max_len: int = 256) -> float:
    if not isinstance(expr, str) or len(expr) > max_len:
        raise ValueError("Expression too long or invalid type")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError as e:
        raise ValueError(f"Invalid syntax: {e}") from e

    def _eval(node):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
            return _eval(node.body)
        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)) and not isinstance(node.value, bool):
                return node.value
            raise ValueError("Only numeric constants allowed")
        if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp) and type(node.op) in _ALLOWED_BINOPS:
            return _ALLOWED_BINOPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.left), _eval(node.right))
        if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp) and type(node.op) in _ALLOWED_UNARYOPS:
            return _ALLOWED_UNARYOPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.operand))
        # Anything else (Call, Attribute, Name, Subscript, Lambda, ...) is rejected
        raise ValueError(f"Disallowed expression element: {type(node).__name__}")

    return _eval(tree)
```

This whitelist approach means `__import__`, `open`, attribute access, and function calls are **structurally impossible** — not just filtered.

---

### 3. OWASP Top 10 mapping for a math engine

| OWASP | Risk in this context | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** | `eval`/`exec` → RCE | AST whitelist evaluator (above); never `eval` |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | No threat model for untrusted input | Treat all input as hostile; defense-in-depth (length + grammar + AST) |
| **A05 Security Misconfiguration** | Debug tracebacks leaking internals | Return generic errors; log details server-side only |
| **A08 Data Integrity** | Unvalidated input reaching evaluator | Validate at the boundary; reject by default |
| **A09 Logging/Monitoring** | No visibility into attack attempts | Log rejected expressions with source, rate, and pattern |
| **A06 Vulnerable Components** | Outdated parser libs | Pin & scan dependencies (e.g., `pip-audit`) |

---

### 4. Denial-of-Service (resource exhaustion) protections

Even "valid" arithmetic can exhaust resources. Add these guards:

```python
MAX_EXPR_LEN   = 256        # characters
MAX_AST_DEPTH  = 20         # nesting depth
MAX_POW_EXP    = 1000       # cap exponents
MAX_INT_BITS   = 10_000     # cap integer size

def guard_pow(base, exp):
    if abs(exp) > MAX_POW_EXP:
        raise ValueError("Exponent too large")
    result = base ** exp
    if isinstance(result, int) and result.bit_length() > MAX_INT_BITS:
        raise ValueError("Result too large")
    return result
```

- **`9**9**9**9`** — a classic "billion laughs" style bomb. Cap exponent magnitude and result size.
- **Deep nesting** — enforce `MAX_AST_DEPTH` during traversal.
- **Huge inputs** — reject by length before parsing.
- **Timeouts** — run evaluation in a worker with a wall-clock limit for untrusted bulk input.

---

### 5. Error handling & logging (A05 / A09)

```python
import logging
logger = logging.getLogger("math.engine")

def handle(expr: str):
    try:
        return {"ok": True, "result": safe_eval(expr)}
    except ValueError as e:
        # Log full detail internally; return generic message externally
        logger.warning("Rejected expression", extra={"expr": expr[:256], "reason": str(e)})
        return {"ok": False, "error": "Invalid expression"}
```

Never echo raw tracebacks or the internal AST to the client — that aids attackers.

---

### 6. Security test cases to add to your suite

```python
import pytest

@pytest.mark.parametrize("payload", [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",
    "().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()",
    "exec('import os')",
    "lambda: 1",
    "[x for x in range(10)]",
    "9**9**9**9",
    "a if b else c",
    "x" * 10_000,
])
def test_rejects_malicious(payload):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        safe_eval(payload)

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr,expected", [
    ("1 + 2", 3),
    ("(3 + 4) * 2 / (5 - 1)", 3.5),
    ("-5 + 2", -3),
    ("2 ** 10", 1024),
])
def test_accepts_arithmetic(expr, expected):
    assert safe_eval(expr) == expected
```

---

### 7. Recommended workflow

1. **Validate syntax** at the boundary (reject non-arithmetic grammar early).
2. **Parse to AST** and **whitelist** node types — never `eval`.
3. **Enforce resource limits** (length, depth, exponent, result size, timeout).
4. **Handle errors safely** — generic messages out, detailed logs in.
5. **Log & monitor** rejected inputs for attack detection.
6. **Pin and scan dependencies** (`pip-audit`, `safety`).
7. **Test adversarially** with the payloads above in CI.

The live probes confirmed the validator correctly rejects injection, file access, and conditional constructs while accepting legitimate arithmetic — combine that grammar gate with the AST whitelist evaluator and resource caps above for defense-in-depth.

## What I verified live (not theoretical)

I ran real expressions through the validator/evaluator pipeline available here. These results are the best illustration of the design you want:

| Expression | Outcome | Lesson |
|---|---|---|
| `(a * b) + sqrt(16) / 2` | ✅ valid | Function calls must be **allow-listed by name**, not free |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected — *"Unexpected character `_`"* | A character/grammar whitelist kills RCE before parsing even completes |
| `1 + + 2 * (3 - )` | ❌ rejected — *"consecutive operator"* ×2 | Struggling on `(3 - )` shows it caught a missing operand |
| `2 ** 3 ** 2` | ❌ rejected — *"Expected expression" at col 4* | `**` is **not** in that grammar. You must decide explicitly whether to allow exponentiation — and if you do, cap it |
| `(1250 * 1.0725) + sqrt(144) / 3` | ✅ `1344.625` | Correct arithmetic + whitelisted `sqrt` |
| `monthly_rate * 250000` (stored var) | ✅ `1510.4166…` | Variables from a **controlled namespace**, not `globals()` |
| `(1 / 0)` | ⚠️ `result: null, type: "number"`, `isError: false` | **Real defect found** — division by zero silently returned `null` while claiming type `number` |

That last row is the most valuable finding: `null` propagating as a "number" is exactly how `NaN`/`None` arithmetic bugs and silent data corruption reach production. Your implementation must raise, not return a sentinel.

## The three-layer design

Never do security with one check. Layer them so a bypass of one still gets caught:

```
untrusted input → [1] grammar/length gate → [2] AST whitelist → [3] bounded evaluator → result
                        ↓ reject            ↓ reject            ↓ raise/timeout
                                        audit log + rate limit
```

### Layer 1 — Gate before parsing (cheap DoS defense)

```python
import re
MAX_LEN, MAX_TOKENS = 512, 100

TOKEN_OK = re.compile(r"^[0-9a-zA-Z_\.\+\-\*/%()\s,<>=!,]*$")

def gate(expr: str) -> str:
    if not isinstance(expr, str):
        raise ValueError("expression must be a string")
    if len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ValueError("expression too long")
    if not TOKEN_OK.match(expr):
        raise ValueError("expression contains disallowed characters")
    if len(re.findall(r"[+\-*/%]", expr)) > MAX_TOKENS:
        raise ValueError("expression too complex")
    return expr
```

This is the layer that rejected `__import__` in my test. Note `.` is allowed for floats — so `.` alone is **not** a defense against attribute access; Layer 2 must handle that. (`ast.Attribute` rejection is what actually stops `x.__class__`.)

### Layer 2 — AST whitelist (the real security boundary)

```python
import ast

ALLOWED_NODES = (
    ast.Expression, ast.BinOp, ast.UnaryOp, ast.Constant,
    ast.Add, ast.Sub, ast.Mult, ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod,
    ast.USub, ast.UAdd, ast.Pow,
    ast.Compare, ast.Lt, ast.LtE, ast.Gt, ast.GtE, ast.Eq, ast.NotEq,
    ast.BoolOp, ast.And, ast.Or, ast.IfExp,
    # ast.Call ONLY via the name check below
)

ALLOWED_FUNCS = {"sqrt": math.sqrt, "abs": abs, "min": min, "max": max,
                 "round": round, "exp": math.exp, "log": math.log}

def validate_ast(expr: str) -> ast.Expression:
    tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if not isinstance(node, ALLOWED_NODES + (ast.Call, ast.Name, ast.Load)):
            raise ValueError(f"disallowed syntax: {type(node).__name__}")
        if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
            if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) or node.func.id not in ALLOWED_FUNCS:
                raise ValueError("function not permitted")
            if node.keywords:
                raise ValueError("keyword arguments not permitted")
        if isinstance(node, ast.Name) and node.id.startswith("_"):
            raise ValueError("private names not permitted")
    return tree
```

Explicitly **not** in the allow-list, and this is the point: `Attribute`, `Subscript`, `Lambda`, `Comprehension`, `ListComp`, `Dict`, `Set`, `NamedExpr` (walrus), `JoinedStr` (f-string), `Starred`, `Await`, `Yield`. Allow-listing means new Python syntax is denied by default — a deny-list would silently admit Python 3.12+ features you never reviewed.

### Layer 3 — Bounded evaluation

```python
class SafeEvaluator:
    def __init__(self, names=None):
        self.names = {"__builtins__": {}}   # empty builtins: no import, no open
        self.names.update(names or {})

    def evaluate(self, expr: str) -> float:
        tree = validate_ast(gate(expr))
        if HAS_POW(tree):
            check_pow_bounds(tree)          # e.g. reject 9**9**9 before computing
        try:
            value = eval(compile(tree, "<expr>", "eval"), self.names, {})
        except ZeroDivisionError:
            raise ValueError("division by zero")     # <- do NOT return None
        except (OverflowError, ValueError):
            raise ValueError("numeric overflow")
        if isinstance(value, complex):
            raise ValueError("complex results not permitted")
        if value != value:                            # NaN check
            raise ValueError("undefined result")
        if abs(value) > 1e308:
            raise ValueError("result out of bounds")
        return float(value)
```

`eval(compile(...))` on a tree that already passed the AST whitelist is defensible; `eval` on a raw string never is. Note the still-present risks: `**` is the classic resource-exhaustion vector (`9**9**9` computes a number with ~370 million digits — badly slow, and it happens *before* any magnitude check). Either drop `ast.Pow` (the validator I tested did exactly that) or pre-check the exponent.

If you'd rather not maintain this, `simpleeval`, `asteval`, or `sympy` with `parse_expr` + explicit symbol/sympy function dicts are battle-tested. `ast.literal_eval` is **not** a substitute — it rejects operators entirely, it isn't a calculator.

## Fixes implied by my test runs

1. **`(1 / 0)` → `null`.** Silent `None` with `type: "number"` breaks every downstream consumer. Raise a typed error.
2. **Inconsistent error locations.** `__import__...` reported *"Column 23"* then *"column 1"*. Don't surface raw parser positions to clients — log them, return a stable generic message.
3. **`2 ** 3 ** 2` unsupported.** Decide deliberately; document it; if enabled, bound it.
4. **`(3 - )` only reported as "consecutive operator."** Fine for a boolean gate, poor for user UX. Return a generic, non-leaking message to the client and the detail to your logs only.

## OWASP Top 10 mapping

| Risk | How this design addresses it |
|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** | AST whitelist blocks `__import__`/`os.system`; no string interpolation into `eval` |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | Layered gates, bounded magnitude, explicit pow policy |
| **A05 Misconfiguration** | `{"__builtins__": {}}`, no `globals()` passthrough |
| **A08 Data Integrity** | Reject `NaN`/`null`/`complex` instead of propagating |
| **A06 Vulnerable Components** | Pin and audit `simpleeval`/`sympy`/`asteval` (they *are* your boundary) |
| **A09 Logging Failures** | Log every rejection with the raw expression + caller identity, rate-limited, never echoing back to the client |
| **A01 Access Control** | Whitelisted variable names only — a caller cannot reach an object it wasn't granted |

## Security test matrix (use these as regression tests)

```python
ATTACKS = [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",
    "().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()",
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",
    "exec('import os')",
    "lambda: 1",
    "[x for x in range(10**9)]",
    "[1,2,3][0]",
    "f'{__builtins__}'",
    "(x := 5)",
    "9**9**9",                      # DoS: must be bounded, not just "allowed"
    "1/0",
    "float('nan')",
    "A" * 10000,
    "\x00\x01",
]
# Each must raise ValueError — never return a value, never 500, never leak a traceback.
```

**Bottom line:** treat the expression string as hostile data, put the security boundary at the AST allow-list (not at character filtering, not at `eval`), fail closed with typed exceptions instead of `null`, and bound every operation that can consume unbounded resources.
